Just the facts in front of me.\n\nDoctor: Have you used any drugs or alcohol recently?\n\nPatient: No, I don't use any drugs. I mean, I might have a beer or two on the weekend, maybe with dinner, but that's about it. Nothing else. I just need a clear head for my work, especially now with everything going on with the data. So, no, nothing recent or out of the ordinary.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been convinced that these data errors are being planted to track your revisions?\n\nPatient: Well, let's see... I'd say it's been about the last two months. Yeah, for about two months now, I've been absolutely certain that people on the project are tracking my revisions and planting those errors.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any period of several days when you felt unusually energized or irritable, with much more energy than usual?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my energy levels are pretty normal for a guy my age. I get tired at the end of the day just like anyone else. I might get a bit annoyed or frustrated when I have to keep fixing the numbers they mess with, sure, but I wouldn't say I'm unusually irritable or, you know, bouncing off the walls with energy. Just my regular routine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a major change in your mood, like feeling very depressed or very high, around the time this started?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I don't feel depressed, and I certainly don't feel high or anything. My mood is pretty much the same as it always is. I'm just focused on my work and trying to keep the data straight. So, no big changes in my mood, really.\n\nDoctor: Has this been affecting your work, relationships, or ability to take care of your daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, it has. I've... I've fallen pretty far behind on my modeling deliverables. It got to the point where the firm actually reassigned pieces of my contract to other people. And, to be honest, I haven't been very useful to the junior analysts I'm supposed to be supervising either. I'm just too caught up in checking the numbers. \n\nAs for at home... I mostly just keep to myself these days. My wife has had to really push me just to keep up with basic routines, you know, like eating and showering. So, yeah, it's definitely taking a toll.\n\nDoctor: Have these beliefs been present for less than one month?\n\nPatient: No, it's been longer than that. I'd say... it's been about two months now. That's how long I've been absolutely certain that people on the project are tracking my revisions and planting those errors in the numbers.\n\nDoctor: Since these beliefs started, have you noticed any decline in your work, relationships, or self-care?\n\nPatient: I think we just talked about this, but yes, it's definitely affecting things. Like I said, I've fallen pretty far behind on my modeling deliverables at work. It got bad enough that the firm had to reassign parts of my contract to other people. And I'm really not much help to the junior analysts I'm supposed to be supervising right now. \n\nAt home, it's... well, I mostly just keep to myself. My wife is the one who has to push me to keep up with basic routines, like eating and showering. So, yes, there's been a noticeable decline across the board.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself acting or speaking in a way that others think is disorganized or hard to follow?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, my wife hasn't said anything about me not making sense, and the people at work haven't mentioned anything like that either. I know what I'm saying, and I think I'm explaining myself fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you had these beliefs continuously for at least six months?\n\nPatient: No, definitely not six months. Like I mentioned a minute ago, it's really only been about two months since I became certain about what they're doing with the numbers. Before that, everything was fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a previous episode like this that lasted for more than one month?\n\nPatient: No, never. This is the first time anything like this has ever happened to me. Before a couple of months ago, everything was completely normal. I've never dealt with anything like this before in my life.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems with your thinking or speech, like trouble keeping your thoughts organized or jumping from one topic to another?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. Like I said a minute ago, I really haven't had any issues with that. My thoughts are perfectly clear and organized. I don't jump around from topic to topic... I know exactly what I'm focused on, and it all makes perfect sense to me. I haven't noticed any problems keeping things straight in my head.\n\nDoctor: Has this belief been about something that could happen in real life, even if you think it’s not true?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, absolutely. I mean, people manipulate data and numbers all the time in the real world. It's not... it's not like I'm talking about ghosts or aliens or something impossible like that. It's just math and records. So, yes, it's definitely something that can and does happen in real life.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
